is entirely analogous.

\displaystyle\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-inflate}}}}}{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\hskip 20.00003pt\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-deflate}}}}}{{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pta}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic0,
Majora & Minora

\displaystyle\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\sup_{n\leq k}\,\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}

\displaystyle~{}={}~\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

The proof of
\square
-deflate
is entirely dual.

\displaystyle\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-idem}}}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}={}\hskip 3.50006pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\hskip 20.00003pt\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-idem}}}}}{{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}={}\hskip 3.50006pt{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic0,
Majora & Minora

\displaystyle\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}~{}={}~\sup_{n\leq k}\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{k}

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{n\leq k}\,\sup_{k\leq i}\,\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{i}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{n\leq i}\,\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{i}

\displaystyle~{}={}~\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

The proof of
\square
-idem
is entirely analogous.

\displaystyle\frac{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptb}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-mono}}}\hskip 20.00003pt\frac{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptb}{{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,b}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}$-mono}}}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic0,
Majora & Minora

By the premise of both rules, we assume that
a\preceq b
, i.e.

\displaystyle\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{b}\rrbracket_{k}

(
asm.
)

holds for all
k\in\omega
.
Then, consider the following:

\displaystyle\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{n\leq k}\,\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\sup_{n\leq k}\,\llbracket{b}\rrbracket_{k}

(by
asm.
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

The proof of
\square
-mono
is entirely analogous.

\displaystyle\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\medcirc}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-comm}}}}}{{\medcirc}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}={}\hskip 3.50006pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{\medcirc}\,a}\hskip 10.00002pt\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\medcirc}{{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-comm}}}}}{{\medcirc}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}={}\hskip 3.50006pt{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\medcirc}\,a}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic0,
Majora & Minora

\displaystyle\llbracket{{\medcirc}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}~{}={}~\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{n+1}

\displaystyle~{}={}~\!\!\!\sup_{n+1\leq k}\,\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{n\leq k}\,\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k+1}

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{n\leq k}\,\llbracket{{\medcirc}\,a}\rrbracket_{k}~{}={}~\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{\medcirc}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

The proof of
{\medcirc}{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}
-comm
is entirely analogous.

\displaystyle{\frac{{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pta}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pta}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-ind}}}\hskip 20.00003pt\frac{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{\medcirc}\,a}{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}$-coind}}}}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic0,
Majora & Minora

\Diamond
-ind
and
\square
-coind
both have double bars, meaning that both rules shall work in both directions.
We thus have four subproofs – two directions for each of the two rules.

\proofsubparagraph

\Diamond
-
IND
from top to bottom:
By the premise of
\Diamond
-ind
(top), we assume
{\medcirc}\,a\preceq a
, i.e.

\displaystyle\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{i+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{\tiny\Cref{tab:semantics}}}}{{\hskip 10.00002pt{}={}\hskip 10.00002pt}}\llbracket{{\medcirc}\,a}\rrbracket_{i}\hskip 10.00002pt{}\sqsubseteq{}\hskip 10.00002pt\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{i}

(
asm.
)

for all
i\in\omega
.
To prove the conclusion of
\Diamond
-ind
(bottom), first notice that

\displaystyle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pta

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\textnormal{iff}\hskip 20.00003pt\forall{n}\colon~\sup_{n\leq k}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\textnormal{iff}\hskip 20.00003pt{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\forall{n,j}\colon~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n+j}~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}}

(by definition of suprema)

Now let
n
be arbitrary but fixed.
We prove that
the latter statement
holds by induction on
j
.
The base case
j=0
is trivial by reflexivity of
\sqsubseteq
.
For the induction step, we have

\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n+j+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\smash{\ref{eq:ind:ass}}}}{{~{}\sqsubseteq{}~}}\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n+j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\smash{\textnormal{I.H.}}}}{{~{}\sqsubseteq{}~}}\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}~.

\proofsubparagraph

\Diamond
-
IND
from bottom to top:
By the premise of
\Diamond
-ind
(bottom), we assume
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\preceq a
, i.e.

\displaystyle\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{i}~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{i}

(
asm.
)

Then, to prove the conclusion of
\Diamond
-ind
(top), consider

\displaystyle\llbracket{{\medcirc}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}

\displaystyle~{}={}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n+1}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\sup_{n\leq k}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}

(by choosing
k=n+1
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}~.

(by
asm.
)

\proofsubparagraph

\square
-
COIND
both directions:
The proofs of
\square
-coind
are entirely dual for both directions.

\displaystyle\frac{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptb}{F\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptF\,b}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F$-mono}}}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic0,
Function Applications & Orbits

The rule
F
-mono
is valid for sequence algebras
if and only if
the endomap
F
on the complete lattice is monotonic.
For a proof, see
\Cref
sec:proof:FMono.

\displaystyle\frac{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptb}{F^{*}a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptF^{*}b}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F^{*}$-mono}}}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic0,
Function Applications & Orbits

By the premise of
F^{*}
-mono
, we assume that

\displaystyle\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{b}\rrbracket_{n}

(
asm.
)

holds for all
n\in\omega
.
We then prove by induction on
k
that for any
n

\displaystyle\forall\,k\colon F^{k}\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~F^{k}\bigl(\llbracket{b}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

(
\dagger
)

holds.
The induction base
k=0
is trivial by
asm.
and reflexivity of
\sqsubseteq
.
As induction hypothesis, assume that
F^{k}\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)\sqsubseteq F^{k}\bigl(\llbracket{b}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)
holds for arbitrary but fixed
k
.
Then we have for the induction step

\displaystyle F^{k}\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~F^{k}\bigl(\llbracket{b}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

(by I.H.)

implies

\displaystyle F\bigl(F^{k}\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~F\bigl(F^{k}\llbracket{b}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

(by monotonicity of
F
)

iff

\displaystyle F^{k+1}\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~F^{k+1}\bigl(\llbracket{b}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

which concludes the proof of (
\dagger
).
We now choose
k=n
in (
\dagger
) and thus obtain

\displaystyle F^{n}\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~F^{n}\bigl(\llbracket{b}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

iff

\displaystyle\llbracket{F^{*}a}\rrbracket_{n}~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{F^{*}b}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

which is the conclusion of
F^{*}
-mono
.

\displaystyle\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F{\medcirc}\,$-comm}}}}}{F\,{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}={}\hskip 3.50006pt{\medcirc}\,F\,a}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic0,
Function Applications & Orbits

\displaystyle\llbracket{{\medcirc}\,Fa}\rrbracket_{n}~{}={}~\llbracket{Fa}\rrbracket_{n+1}~{}={}~F\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n+1}\bigr)~{}={}~F\bigl(\llbracket{{\medcirc}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}={}~\llbracket{F{\medcirc}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}

(all by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$FF^{*}$-comm}}}}}{F\,F^{*}a\hskip 3.50006pt{}={}\hskip 3.50006ptF^{*}F\,a}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic0,
Function Applications & Orbits

\displaystyle\llbracket{F\,F^{*}a}\rrbracket_{n}~{}={}~F\,\bigl(\llbracket{F^{*}a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle~{}={}~F\,\bigl(F\,^{n}\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}={}~F\,^{n}\bigl(F\,\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle~{}={}~F\,^{n}\bigl(\llbracket{F\,a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}={}~\llbracket{F^{*}F\,a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}iter}}}}}{{\medcirc}\,F^{*}a\hskip 3.50006pt{}={}\hskip 3.50006ptF\,F^{*}{\medcirc}\,a}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic0,
Function Applications & Orbits

\displaystyle\llbracket{{\medcirc}\,F^{*}a}\rrbracket_{n}~{}={}~\llbracket{F^{*}a}\rrbracket_{n+1}~{}={}~F\,^{n+1}\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n+1}\bigr)

\displaystyle~{}={}~F\,^{n+1}\bigl(\llbracket{{\medcirc}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}={}~F\,\Bigl(F\,^{n}\bigl(\llbracket{{\medcirc}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle~{}={}~F\,\Bigl(\llbracket{F^{*}a}\rrbracket_{n}\Bigr)~{}={}~\llbracket{F\,F^{*}{\medcirc}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle\frac{F\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pta}{F^{*}a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pta}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F$-ind}}}\hskip 20.00003pt\frac{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptF\,a}{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptF^{*}a}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F$-coind}}}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic0,
Function Applications & Orbits

\proofsubparagraph

Proof of
\boldsymbol{F}
-
IND
:

By the premise of
F
-ind
, we assume that

\displaystyle\llbracket{F\,a}\rrbracket_{n}~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}

\displaystyle\textnormal{implies}\hskip 20.00003ptF\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)
(
asm.
)

holds for all
n\in\omega
.
We then prove by induction on
k
that for any
n

\displaystyle\forall\,k\colon F^{k}\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}

(
\dagger
)

holds.
The induction base
k=0
is trivial by
asm.
and reflexivity of
\sqsubseteq
.
As induction hypothesis, assume that
F^{k}\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)\sqsubseteq\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}
holds for arbitrary but fixed
k
.
Then we have for the induction step

\displaystyle F^{k}\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by I.H.)

implies

\displaystyle F\bigl(F^{k}\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{mono}}}{{\sqsubseteq}}F\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{.}{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}\small\textsc{asm.}}}}{{\sqsubseteq}}\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by monotonicity of
F
and
asm.
)

implies

\displaystyle F\bigl(F^{k}\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}

iff

\displaystyle F^{k+1}\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}

which concludes the proof of (
\dagger
).
We now choose
k=n
in (
\dagger
) and thus obtain

\displaystyle F^{n}\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}

iff

\displaystyle\llbracket{F^{*}a}\rrbracket_{n}~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

which is the conclusion of
F
-ind
.

\proofsubparagraph

Proof of
\boldsymbol{F}
-
COIND
:
The proof of
F
-coind
is entirely dual.
∎

Appendix B
Appendix: Axioms Pertaining to Properties of Functions

In this section, we provide omitted proofs on axioms pertaining to properties of functions like monotonicity and continuity.

B.1
Monotonicity

\displaystyle\frac{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptb}{F\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptF\,b}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F$-mono}}}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic0,
Function Applications & Orbits

Theorem 28

(Precise Capturing of Monotonicity)
.

The axiom
F
-mono
is valid for sequence algebras
if and only
if the endomap
F
on the underlying complete lattice is monotonic.

Proof.

We prove both directions.

\proofsubparagraph

If
\boldsymbol{F}
is monotonic, then
\boldsymbol{F}
-
MONO
is valid:

Consider the following:

\displaystyle\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{b}\rrbracket_{n}

(by assuming the premise of
F
-mono
)

implies

\displaystyle F\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~F\bigl(\llbracket{b}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

(by assuming monotonicity of
F
)

iff

\displaystyle\llbracket{F\,a}\rrbracket_{n}~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{F\,b}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\proofsubparagraph

If
\boldsymbol{F}
-
MONO
is valid, then
\boldsymbol{F}
is monotonic:
Let
\ell
and
m
be two arbitrary lattice elements with
\ell\sqsubseteq m
.
From these two, form the two constant sequences
\overline{\ell}=\ell~\ell~\ell~\ell\ldots{}
and
\overline{m}=m~m~m~m\ldots{}
.
Then consider the following:

\displaystyle\ell\sqsubseteq m

iff

\displaystyle\forall n\colon\llbracket{\,\overline{\ell}\,}\rrbracket_{n}\sqsubseteq\llbracket{\,\overline{m}\,}\rrbracket_{n}

(by definition of
\overline{\ell}
and
\overline{m}
)

implies

\displaystyle\forall n\colon\llbracket{F\,\overline{\ell}\,}\rrbracket_{n}\sqsubseteq\llbracket{F\,\overline{m}\,}\rrbracket_{n}

(by assuming validity of
F
-mono
)

iff

\displaystyle\forall n\colon F\bigl(\llbracket{\,\overline{\ell}\,}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~F\bigl(\llbracket{\,\overline{m}\,}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

iff

\displaystyle F(\ell)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~F(m)

(by definition of
\overline{\ell}
and
\overline{m}
)

thus proving that
F
is monotonic.
∎

B.2
\boldsymbol{\omega}
-continuity and
\boldsymbol{\omega}
-cocontinuity

\displaystyle\frac{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{\medcirc}\,a}{F\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,F\,a}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$\omega$-cont}}}\hskip 20.00003pt\frac{{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pta}{{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,F\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptF\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$\omega$-cocont}}}

\Cref
sec:aic1,
Continuous Functions

Theorem 29

(Precise Capturing of
\omega
-(co)continuity)
.

{romanenumerate}

The axiom
\omega
-cont
is valid for sequence algebras
if and only
if the endomap
F
on the underlying complete lattice is
\omega
-continuous.

The axiom
\omega
-cocont
is valid for sequence algebras
if and only
if the endomap
F
on the underlying complete lattice is
\omega
-cocontinuous.

Proof.

We only prove the case for
\omega
-cont
. The case for
\omega
-cocont
is entirely dual.

\proofsubparagraph

If
\boldsymbol{F}
is
\boldsymbol{\omega}
-continuous, then
\boldsymbol{\omega}
-
CONT
is valid:

By the premise of
\omega
-cont
, we assume that

\displaystyle\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{{\medcirc}\,a}\rrbracket_{k}~{}\hskip 10.00002pt{}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\text{\tiny by \Cref{tab:semantics}}}}}{{=}}{}\hskip 10.00002pt{}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k+1}

holds for any
k\in\omega
, which captures precisely that
\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}\bigr)_{k\in\omega}
is an ascending chain.
If
\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}\bigr)_{k\in\omega}
is an ascending chain, it is clear that for any
n\in\omega
also

\displaystyle\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}\bigr)_{n\leq k}\hskip 20.00003pt\textnormal{is an ascending chain.}

(
asm.
)

Then consider the following:

\displaystyle\llbracket{F{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}~{}={}~F\bigl(\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle~{}={}~F\biggl(\sup_{n\leq k}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}\biggr)

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\sup_{n\leq k}~F\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

(by
asm.
and assuming
\omega
-continuity of
F
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{n\leq k}~\llbracket{Fa}\rrbracket_{n}~{}={}~\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,Fa}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\proofsubparagraph

If
\boldsymbol{\omega}
-
CONT
is valid, then
\boldsymbol{F}
is
\boldsymbol{\omega}
-continuous:
Assume that
a
is a variable that stands for an arbitrary ascending chain, i.e.
\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)_{n\in\omega}
is an arbitrary ascending chain.
Then for any
n\in\omega
, we have

\displaystyle\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}{\hskip 20.00003pt{}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\text{\tiny$a$ ascending}}}}{{\sqsubseteq}}{}\hskip 20.00003pt}\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n+1}~{}\hskip 10.00002pt{}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\text{\tiny by \Cref{tab:semantics}}}}}{{=}}{}\hskip 10.00002pt{}~\llbracket{{\medcirc}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}

(
asm.
)

thus fulfilling the premise of
\omega
-cont
.

We now have to prove that
F\bigl(\sup_{n\in\omega}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)\sqsubseteq\sup_{n\in\omega}~F\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)
.
Assuming that
\omega
-cont
is valid, we reason for this as follows:

\displaystyle F\biggl(\sup_{n\in\omega}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\biggr)

\displaystyle~{}={}~F\biggl(\sup_{0\leq n}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\biggr)

\displaystyle~{}={}~F\bigl(\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{0}\bigr)

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\llbracket{F\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{0}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,F\,a}\rrbracket_{0}

(by
asm.
and assuming validity of
\omega
-cont
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{0\leq n}~\llbracket{F\,a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{0\leq n}~F\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{n\in\omega}F\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

∎

B.3
Countable Continuity and Countable Cocontinuity

\displaystyle\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}c-cont}}}}}{F\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,F\,a}\hskip 20.00003pt\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}c-cocont}}}}}{{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,F\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptF\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}

\Cref
sec:aic1,
Continuous Functions

Theorem 30

(Precise Capturing of Countable (Co)continuity)
.

{romanenumerate}

The axiom
c-cont
is valid for sequence algebras
if and only
if the endomap
F
on the underlying complete lattice is countably continuous.

The axiom
c-cocont
is valid for sequence algebras
if and only
if the endomap
F
on the underlying complete lattice is countably cocontinuous.

Proof.

We only prove the case for
c-cont
. The case for
c-cocont
is entirely dual.

\proofsubparagraph

If
\boldsymbol{F}
is countably continuous, then
C-CONT
is valid:

For any
a
, clearly the set

\displaystyle\left\{\,{\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}}~\middle|~{n\leq k}\,\right\}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{is countable}

(
asm.
)

Now, consider the following:

\displaystyle\llbracket{F{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}~{}={}~F\bigl(\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle~{}={}~F\biggl(\sup_{n\leq k}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{k}\biggr)

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\sup_{n\leq k}~F\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

(by assuming countable continuity of
F
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{n\leq k}~\llbracket{Fa}\rrbracket_{n}~{}={}~\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,Fa}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\proofsubparagraph

If
C-CONT
is valid, then
\boldsymbol{F}
is countably continuous:
Assume that
a
is a variable that enumerates an arbitrary countable set of lattice elements
\left\{\,{\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}}~\middle|~{n\in\omega}\,\right\}
.
For this arbitrary set, we have to prove that
F\bigl(\sup_{n\in\omega}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)\sqsubseteq\sup_{n\in\omega}~F\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)
.
Assuming that
c-cont
is valid, we reason for this as follows:

\displaystyle F\biggl(\sup_{n\in\omega}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\biggr)

\displaystyle~{}={}~F\biggl(\sup_{0\leq n}~\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\biggr)

\displaystyle~{}={}~F\bigl(\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{0}\bigr)

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\llbracket{F\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\rrbracket_{0}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\llbracket{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,F\,a}\rrbracket_{0}

(by
asm.
and assuming validity of
c-cont
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{0\leq n}~\llbracket{F\,a}\rrbracket_{n}

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{0\leq n}~F\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

(by
\Cref
tab:semantics)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{n\in\omega}F\bigl(\llbracket{a}\rrbracket_{n}\bigr)

∎

Appendix C
Appendix: Soundness of
\boldsymbol{{\mathsf{AIC}}_{1}}

In this section, we prove the soundness of the
{\mathsf{AIC}}_{1}
axiom system (see
\Cref
fig:axioms-aic1).

See
13

Proof.

All axioms in
{\mathsf{AIC}}_{0}
are included in
{\mathsf{AIC}}_{1}
and all axioms in
{\mathsf{AIC}}_{0}
are valid for sequence algebras.
Every axiom in
{\mathsf{AIC}}_{1}\setminus{\mathsf{AIC}}_{0}
(see
\Cref
fig:axioms-aic1) is finitely derivable from
{\mathsf{AIC}}_{0}
.
Thus, in the following we only have to show these derivations for the axioms in
\Cref
fig:axioms-aic1.

For the sake of being explicit, we will (un)fold the definitions of inequations
a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptb
into equations by the following rules:

\displaystyle{\frac{b\curlyvee a\hskip 3.23753pt{}={}\hskip 3.23753pta}{b\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pta}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}sugar}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt{\frac{b\hskip 3.23753pt{}={}\hskip 3.23753pta\curlywedge b}{b\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pta}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}sugar}}}}\hskip 18.49988pt{\frac{b\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pta}{b\curlyvee a\hskip 3.23753pt{}={}\hskip 3.23753pta}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}desugar}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt{\frac{b\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pta}{b\hskip 3.23753pt{}={}\hskip 3.23753pta\curlywedge a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}desugar}}}}

Note that these are really just purely presentational rewritings – not inference rules or quasiequations.
We hence use a dotted inference bar to make that distinction.

We now go over the axioms of
\Cref
fig:axioms-aic1 and show derivations from
{\mathsf{AIC}}_{0}
and already-derived axioms in
{\mathsf{AIC}}_{1}
.

\displaystyle\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}reflex}}}}}{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pta}

rule for

\displaystyle{\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$\curlyvee$-comm}}}}}{b\curlyvee a\hskip 1.57503pt{}={}\hskip 1.57503pta\curlyvee b}\enskip\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}eq-reflex}}}}}{a\curlyvee b\hskip 1.57503pt{}={}\hskip 1.57503pta\curlyvee b}}{(b\curlyvee a)\curlyvee(a\curlyvee b)\hskip 1.57503pt{}={}\hskip 1.57503pt(a\curlyvee b)\curlyvee(a\curlyvee b)}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Left={{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}cong}\hskip 3.15pt}}\hskip 8.04996pt\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$\curlyvee$-idem}}}}}{(a\curlyvee b)\curlyvee(a\curlyvee b)\hskip 1.57503pt{}={}\hskip 1.57503pta\curlyvee b}}{(b\curlyvee a)\curlyvee(a\curlyvee b)\hskip 2.81752pt{}={}\hskip 2.81752pta\curlyvee b}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}eq-trans}}}}{b\curlyvee a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pta\curlyvee b}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}sugar}}}}

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\frac{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{\medcirc}\,a}{F^{*}a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503ptF^{*}{\medcirc}\,a}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 3.15pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F^{*}$-mono}}}}{F\,F^{*}a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752ptF\,F^{*}{\medcirc}\,a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F$-mono}}}}{F\,F^{*}a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pt\hbox{\pagecolor{DodgerBlue3!30!white}${\medcirc}\,F^{*}a$}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}iter}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\frac{\frac{\frac{{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pta}{F^{*}{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503ptF^{*}a}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 3.15pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F^{*}$-mono}}}}{F\,F^{*}{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752ptF\,F^{*}a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F$-mono}}}}{\hbox{\pagecolor{DodgerBlue3!30!white}${\medcirc}\,F^{*}a$}\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753ptF\,F^{*}a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}iter}}}

\displaystyle\frac{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptF\,a\hskip 10.00002pta\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{\medcirc}\,a}{F^{*}a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{\medcirc}\,F^{*}a}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}orbit-asc}}}\hskip 20.00003pt\frac{{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pta\hskip 10.00002ptF\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pta}{{\medcirc}\,F^{*}a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptF^{*}a}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}orbit-desc}}}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic1,
Additional Function Application & Orbit Axioms

We only show a derivation for
orbit-asc
; the one for
orbit-desc
is analogous.

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}{asm.}}}}}}}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503ptF\,a}\enskip\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}{asm.}}}}}}}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{\medcirc}\,a}}{F\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503ptF\,{\medcirc}\,a}\enskip\textsc{Right={\hskip 2.25pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F$-mono}}}}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503ptF\,{\medcirc}\,a}\enskip\textsc{Right={\hskip 2.25pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}trans}}}}{F^{*}a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503ptF^{*}F\,{\medcirc}\,a}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 3.15pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F^{*}$-mono}}}}{F^{*}a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752pt\hbox{\pagecolor{DodgerBlue3!30!white}$F\,F^{*}{\medcirc}\,a$}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$FF^{*}$-comm}}}}{F^{*}a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pt\hbox{\pagecolor{DodgerBlue3!30!white}${\medcirc}\,F^{*}a$}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}iter}}}

\displaystyle\frac{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptb\hskip 10.00002ptF\,b\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptb}{F^{*}a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptb}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F^{*}$-introL}}}\hskip 20.00003pt\frac{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptF\,a\hskip 10.00002pta\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptb}{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptF^{*}b}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F^{*}$-introR}}}

\Cref
fig:axioms-aic1,
Additional Function Application & Orbit Axioms

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{{\frac{\textnormal{\small\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}\footnotesize{asm.}}}}}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503ptb}}}{F^{*}a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752ptF^{*}b}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Left={{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F^{*}$-mono}\hskip 4.5pt}}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{{\frac{\textnormal{\small\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}\footnotesize{asm.}}}}}{Fb\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503ptb}}}{F^{*}b\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752ptb}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F$-ind}}}}{F^{*}a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753ptb}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}trans}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\frac{\frac{{\frac{\textnormal{\small\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}\footnotesize{asm.}}}}}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503ptFa}}}{a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752ptF^{*}a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Left={{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F$-coind}\hskip 4.5pt}}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{{\frac{\textnormal{\small\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}\footnotesize{asm.}}}}}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503ptb}}}{F^{*}a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752ptF^{*}b}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$F^{*}$-mono}}}}{a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753ptF^{*}b}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}trans}}}

∎

Appendix D
Appendix: Majora-Minora Alternation Depth Hierarchy Collapse

Just like in LTL
[
3
, Figure 5.7, absorption laws]
, the depth of alternating
\Diamond
’s and
\square
’s collapses at level 2 in the following sense:

Theorem 31

(Majora-Minora Alternation Depth Hierarchy Collapse)
.

The majora-minora alternation depth hierarchy in AIC collapses at level 2, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{1}\,\mathsf{M}_{2}\,\cdots\,\mathsf{M_{k}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,\cdots\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\square$}}}\,\cdots\,{{\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\square$}}}\,a

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt{}={}\hskip 10.00002pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 20.00003pt\textnormal{and}

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{1}\,\mathsf{M}_{2}\,\cdots\,\mathsf{M_{k}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\square$}}}\,\cdots\,{{\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,\cdots\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt{}={}\hskip 10.00002pt{{\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a

hold, where the leading
\mathsf{M}_{1}\,\cdots\,\mathsf{M}_{k}
are any sequence of
\Diamond
’s and
\square
’s.

Proof.

We only prove the first equation; the other one is analogous.
Consider
b=\mathsf{M}_{1}\,\mathsf{M}_{2}\,\cdots\,\mathsf{M_{k}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,\cdots\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,\cdots\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a
.
First, by repeatedly applying
\Diamond
-idem
and
\square
-idem
, we can bring
b
into the form
\mathsf{M}_{1}\,\mathsf{M}_{2}\,\cdots\,\mathsf{M_{k}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a
.

Consider now the innermost subterm
\mathsf{M_{k}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a
.
If
\mathsf{M_{k}}={\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}
, then we can again apply
\Diamond
-idem
to obtain the equivalent term
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a
.
If
\mathsf{M_{k}}={{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}
, we can also obtain the equivalent term
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a
as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-deflate}}}}}{{{\raisebox{-0.71747pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.71747pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 2.8681pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.8681pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.71747pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\hskip 10.00002pt\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-asc}}}}}{{{\raisebox{-0.61247pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 2.57646pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.57646pt{\medcirc}\,{{\raisebox{-0.61247pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.61247pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 2.57646pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.57646pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{\medcirc}\,{{\raisebox{-0.61247pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 2.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-mono}}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.61247pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 2.57646pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.57646pt\hbox{\pagecolor{DodgerBlue3!30!white}${\medcirc}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.61247pt}{$\square$}}}\,a$}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 3.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\medcirc}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-comm}}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.71747pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 2.8681pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.8681pt{{\raisebox{-0.71747pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.71747pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}$-coind}}}}{{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}={}\hskip 3.50006pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}antisymm}}}

So in any case we could show that
\mathsf{M_{k}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a={\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a
, thus obtaining overall the term
\mathsf{M}_{1}\,\mathsf{M}_{2}\,\cdots\,\mathsf{M_{k-1}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,\cdots\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,\cdots\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a
, which is equivalent to
b
but has one fewer majorum or minorum.
Repeatedly applying this proof procedure to
\mathsf{M_{k-1}}
through
\mathsf{M_{1}}
finally leaves us with the desired equation
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a=b
.
∎

From this observation, we can immediately conclude that
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a
is flat (and so is
{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a
):

\displaystyle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a

(expressing that
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a
is flat)

iff

\displaystyle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a

(by
\Diamond
-idem
)

iff

\displaystyle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a~.

(by alternation depth collapse)

Appendix E
Appendix: Further Derivable Axioms

In this section, we present and derive further useful axioms that are derivable from
{\mathsf{AIC}}_{1}
.

\displaystyle\frac{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}{{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\equiv{}\hskip 3.50006pta}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\medcirc}$-flat}}}

This axiom describes that both
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\preceq{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a
as well as
{\medcirc}\,a=a
capture equivalently that
a
is flat.
For the direction from top to bottom, consider the following derivation:

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}{asm.}}}}}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{{\raisebox{-0.40498pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pta}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 3.15pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.56697pt}{$\square$}}}$-elim}}}}{{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752pta}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\medcirc}$-ind}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}{asm.}}}}}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{{\raisebox{-0.40498pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{{\raisebox{-0.40498pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 3.15pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-elim}}}}{a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752pt{\medcirc}\,a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-coind}}}}{{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\equiv{}\hskip 3.23753pta}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}antisymm}}}

From bottom to top, consider the following derivation:

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}{asm.}}}}}}}{{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}={}\hskip 1.57503pta}}{{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pta}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Left={{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}weakenR}\hskip 3.15pt}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752pta}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Left={{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\medcirc}$-ind}\hskip 4.5pt}}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}{asm.}}}}}}}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}={}\hskip 1.57503pt{\medcirc}\,a}}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{\medcirc}\,a}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 3.15pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}weakenR}}}}{a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752pt{{\raisebox{-0.69296pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\medcirc}$-coind}}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pt{{\raisebox{-0.83246pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}cut}}}

\displaystyle\frac{a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pt{\medcirc}\,a}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pt{{\raisebox{-0.83246pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-asc-flat}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\frac{{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pta}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.83246pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pt{{\raisebox{-0.83246pt}{$\square$}}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.83246pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-desc-flat}}}

This axiom describes that the majorum of an ascending chain is flat; and dually that the minorum of a descending chain is flat.
For derivations, consider the following:

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}{asm.}}}}}}}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{\medcirc}\,a}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{\medcirc}\,a}\enskip\textsc{Right={\hskip 2.25pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-mono}}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt\hbox{\pagecolor{DodgerBlue3!30!white}${\medcirc}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a$}}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 3.15pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\medcirc}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-comm}}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752pt{{\raisebox{-0.69296pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-coind}}}}{\hbox{\pagecolor{DodgerBlue3!30!white}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a$}\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pt{{\raisebox{-0.83246pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-idem}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}{asm.}}}}}}}{{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pta}}{{{\raisebox{-0.40498pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\medcirc}\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{{\raisebox{-0.40498pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\enskip\textsc{Right={\hskip 2.25pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.40498pt}{$\square$}}}$-mono}}}}{\hbox{\pagecolor{DodgerBlue3!30!white}${\medcirc}\,{{\raisebox{-0.40498pt}{$\square$}}}\,a$}\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{{\raisebox{-0.40498pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 3.15pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\medcirc}{{\raisebox{-0.56697pt}{$\square$}}}$-comm}}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.69296pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752pt{{\raisebox{-0.69296pt}{$\square$}}}\,a}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-coind}}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.83246pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pt\hbox{\pagecolor{DodgerBlue3!30!white}${{\raisebox{-0.83246pt}{$\square$}}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.83246pt}{$\square$}}}\,a$}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-idem}}}

\displaystyle\frac{a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006ptb}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 3.50006pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.50006pt{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}\hskip 10.00002pt\textsc{right={\hskip 5.0pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}$\,/\,${{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-mono}}}

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}{\color[rgb]{0.435,0.655,0.875}{asm.}}}}}}}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503ptb}}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Left={{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-introR}\hskip 3.15pt}}}{{{\raisebox{-0.69296pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752pt{{\raisebox{-0.69296pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Left={{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-mono}\hskip 4.5pt}}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-desc}}}}}{{\medcirc}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}}{{{\raisebox{-0.40498pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\medcirc}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{{\raisebox{-0.40498pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 3.15pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.56697pt}{$\square$}}}$-mono}}}}{\hbox{\pagecolor{DodgerBlue3!30!white}${\medcirc}\,{{\raisebox{-0.69296pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b$}\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752pt{{\raisebox{-0.69296pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\medcirc}{{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-comm}}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{{\raisebox{-0.83246pt}{$\square$}}}\,a\hskip 3.23753pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 3.23753pt{{\raisebox{-0.83246pt}{$\square$}}}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 4.5pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-introL}}}

\displaystyle\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-over-$\curlyvee$}}}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,(a\curlyvee b)\hskip 3.50006pt{}={}\hskip 3.50006pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a~{}\curlyvee{}~{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\frac{\textnormal{\small{\raisebox{-1.93748pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${{\raisebox{-0.80997pt}{$\square$}}}$-over-$\curlywedge$}}}}}{{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,(a\curlywedge b)\hskip 3.50006pt{}={}\hskip 3.50006pt{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,a~{}\curlywedge{}~{{\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\square$}}}\,b}

We only show a derivation for
\Diamond
-over-
\curlyvee
; the one for
\square
-over-
\curlywedge
is analogous.
We prove
\preceq
and
\succeq
separately.

-

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\frac{\,}{a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Left={{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-introR}\hskip 3.15pt}}\hskip 8.04996pt\frac{\,}{b\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{right={\hskip 3.15pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-introR}}}}{a\curlyvee b\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 2.81752pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\curlyvee{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Left={{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$\curlyvee$-introL}\hskip 4.5pt}}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\frac{\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-desc}}}}}{{\medcirc}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a}\enskip\frac{\textnormal{\footnotesize{\raisebox{-1.72221pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}${\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}$-desc}}}}}{{\medcirc}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}}{{\medcirc}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\curlyvee{\medcirc}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b\hskip 1.57503pt{}\preceq{}\hskip 1.57503pt{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\curlyvee{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right={\hskip 3.15pt{}{\color[rgb]{0.5625,0.5625,0.5625}$\curlyvee$-introL}}}}{{\medcirc}\,({\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a\curlyvee{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,b)\hskip 2.81752pt{}\preceq{}\hskip
