8

8

Given a well-founded tree
T
, a property
P(n)
holds for all nodes
n
of
T
whenever the following can be shown: for all nodes
n
, if
P(m)
holds for all children
m
of
n
, then
P(n)
holds.
Thus, fix a node of
D
. If the node is a leaf labelled with one of the premises
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}s^{{\kern-5.90079pt{j}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}s^{{\kern-5.90079pt{j}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}s^{{\kern-3.69469pt{j}\kern 2.06718pt}}_{{\kern-1.7172pt\kern 2.06718pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}s^{{\kern-3.10406pt{j}\kern 1.47656pt}}_{{\kern-1.12657pt\kern 1.47656pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}t^{{\kern-5.22266pt{j}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}t^{{\kern-5.22266pt{j}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}t^{{\kern-3.22pt{j}\kern 1.5925pt}}_{{\kern-1.24251pt\kern 1.5925pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}t^{{\kern-2.765pt{j}\kern 1.1375pt}}_{{\kern-0.7875pt\kern 1.1375pt}}}
(
j\in I
), then
\textup{{q}}^{\prime}
is the quasiequation
(\bigwedge_{i\in I}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-5.35158pt{i}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-5.35158pt{i}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-3.3024pt{i}\kern 2.06718pt}}_{{\kern-1.7172pt\kern 2.06718pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-2.71178pt{i}\kern 1.47656pt}}_{{\kern-1.12657pt\kern 1.47656pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-4.67345pt{i}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-4.67345pt{i}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-2.82771pt{i}\kern 1.5925pt}}_{{\kern-1.24251pt\kern 1.5925pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-2.37271pt{i}\kern 1.1375pt}}_{{\kern-0.7875pt\kern 1.1375pt}}})\Longrightarrow\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}s^{{\kern-5.90079pt{j}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}s^{{\kern-5.90079pt{j}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}s^{{\kern-3.69469pt{j}\kern 2.06718pt}}_{{\kern-1.7172pt\kern 2.06718pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}s^{{\kern-3.10406pt{j}\kern 1.47656pt}}_{{\kern-1.12657pt\kern 1.47656pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}t^{{\kern-5.22266pt{j}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}t^{{\kern-5.22266pt{j}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}t^{{\kern-3.22pt{j}\kern 1.5925pt}}_{{\kern-1.24251pt\kern 1.5925pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}t^{{\kern-2.765pt{j}\kern 1.1375pt}}_{{\kern-0.7875pt\kern 1.1375pt}}}
, which holds in every AIC-structure, so
{\mathcal{A}}\models\textup{{q}}^{\prime}
. Otherwise the node is a point of branching

\frac{\frac{\phantom{D_{l}}~\vdots~D_{l}}{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}u^{{\kern-3.82793pt{l}\kern 2.57605pt}}_{{\kern-2.32605pt\kern 2.57605pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}u^{{\kern-3.82793pt{l}\kern 2.57605pt}}_{{\kern-2.32605pt\kern 2.57605pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}u^{{\kern-2.76884pt{l}\kern 1.80324pt}}_{{\kern-1.55324pt\kern 1.80324pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}u^{{\kern-2.25363pt{l}\kern 1.28802pt}}_{{\kern-1.03802pt\kern 1.28802pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}v^{{\kern-3.59457pt{l}\kern 2.3427pt}}_{{\kern-2.0927pt\kern 2.3427pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}v^{{\kern-3.59457pt{l}\kern 2.3427pt}}_{{\kern-2.0927pt\kern 2.3427pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}v^{{\kern-2.60548pt{l}\kern 1.63988pt}}_{{\kern-1.38988pt\kern 1.63988pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}v^{{\kern-2.13695pt{l}\kern 1.17134pt}}_{{\kern-0.92134pt\kern 1.17134pt}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{3.84995pt}{(for $l,l^{\prime}\in L$)}}}{{\cdots}}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\phantom{D_{l^{\prime}}}~\vdots~D_{l^{\prime}}}{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{l^{\prime}}}}}u^{{\kern-4.94667pt{l^{\prime}}\kern 2.57605pt}}_{{\kern-2.32605pt\kern 2.57605pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l^{\prime}}}}}u^{{\kern-4.94667pt{l^{\prime}}\kern 2.57605pt}}_{{\kern-2.32605pt\kern 2.57605pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l^{\prime}}}}}u^{{\kern-3.88759pt{l^{\prime}}\kern 1.80324pt}}_{{\kern-1.55324pt\kern 1.80324pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l^{\prime}}}}}u^{{\kern-3.37238pt{l^{\prime}}\kern 1.28802pt}}_{{\kern-1.03802pt\kern 1.28802pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{l^{\prime}}}}}v^{{\kern-4.71332pt{l^{\prime}}\kern 2.3427pt}}_{{\kern-2.0927pt\kern 2.3427pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l^{\prime}}}}}v^{{\kern-4.71332pt{l^{\prime}}\kern 2.3427pt}}_{{\kern-2.0927pt\kern 2.3427pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l^{\prime}}}}}v^{{\kern-3.72423pt{l^{\prime}}\kern 1.63988pt}}_{{\kern-1.38988pt\kern 1.63988pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l^{\prime}}}}}v^{{\kern-3.25569pt{l^{\prime}}\kern 1.17134pt}}_{{\kern-0.92134pt\kern 1.17134pt}}}}}{s^{\prime}=t^{\prime}}

(52)

and, by induction hypothesis,
{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies all the quasiequations
(\bigwedge_{i\in I}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-5.35158pt{i}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-5.35158pt{i}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-3.3024pt{i}\kern 2.06718pt}}_{{\kern-1.7172pt\kern 2.06718pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-2.71178pt{i}\kern 1.47656pt}}_{{\kern-1.12657pt\kern 1.47656pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-4.67345pt{i}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-4.67345pt{i}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-2.82771pt{i}\kern 1.5925pt}}_{{\kern-1.24251pt\kern 1.5925pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-2.37271pt{i}\kern 1.1375pt}}_{{\kern-0.7875pt\kern 1.1375pt}}})\Longrightarrow\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}u^{{\kern-6.1974pt{l}\kern 4.64478pt}}_{{\kern-4.29478pt\kern 4.64478pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}u^{{\kern-6.1974pt{l}\kern 4.64478pt}}_{{\kern-4.29478pt\kern 4.64478pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}u^{{\kern-3.67642pt{l}\kern 2.52454pt}}_{{\kern-2.17455pt\kern 2.52454pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}u^{{\kern-2.95512pt{l}\kern 1.80324pt}}_{{\kern-1.45325pt\kern 1.80324pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}v^{{\kern-5.71613pt{l}\kern 4.1635pt}}_{{\kern-3.8135pt\kern 4.1635pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}v^{{\kern-5.71613pt{l}\kern 4.1635pt}}_{{\kern-3.8135pt\kern 4.1635pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}v^{{\kern-3.44772pt{l}\kern 2.29584pt}}_{{\kern-1.94585pt\kern 2.29584pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{l}}}}v^{{\kern-2.79176pt{l}\kern 1.63988pt}}_{{\kern-1.28989pt\kern 1.63988pt}}}
where
l\in L
. Since (
52
) is a substitution instance of either some axiom of equational logic or of some axiom in
\mathsf{Ax}
, and
{\mathcal{A}}\models\mathsf{Ax}
, it follows that
{\mathcal{A}}\models\textup{{q}}^{\prime}

(
\Longleftarrow
) Let
\textup{{q}}=(\bigwedge_{i\in I}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-5.35158pt{i}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-5.35158pt{i}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-3.3024pt{i}\kern 2.06718pt}}_{{\kern-1.7172pt\kern 2.06718pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-2.71178pt{i}\kern 1.47656pt}}_{{\kern-1.12657pt\kern 1.47656pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-4.67345pt{i}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-4.67345pt{i}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-2.82771pt{i}\kern 1.5925pt}}_{{\kern-1.24251pt\kern 1.5925pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-2.37271pt{i}\kern 1.1375pt}}_{{\kern-0.7875pt\kern 1.1375pt}}})\Longrightarrow s=t
and suppose that
{\mathcal{A}}\models\textup{{q}}
for every AIC-structure
{\mathcal{A}}
satisfying
\mathsf{Ax}
.
We construct an AIC-structure
{\mathcal{A}}
that satisfies the property that if
{\mathcal{A}}\models\textup{{q}}
, then
\mathsf{Ax}\vdash_{\mathsf{wf}}\textup{{q}}
: let
{\approx}\subseteq\mathsf{Terms}^{2}
be the relation defined such that
u\approx v
if and only if
\mathsf{Ax}\vdash_{\mathsf{wf}}(\bigwedge_{i\in I}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-5.35158pt{i}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-5.35158pt{i}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-3.3024pt{i}\kern 2.06718pt}}_{{\kern-1.7172pt\kern 2.06718pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-2.71178pt{i}\kern 1.47656pt}}_{{\kern-1.12657pt\kern 1.47656pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-4.67345pt{i}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-4.67345pt{i}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-2.82771pt{i}\kern 1.5925pt}}_{{\kern-1.24251pt\kern 1.5925pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-2.37271pt{i}\kern 1.1375pt}}_{{\kern-0.7875pt\kern 1.1375pt}}})\Longrightarrow u=v
.
Since
\mathsf{Ax}
includes equational logic (
\cref
fig:equational logic),
\approx
is a congruence.
This allows us to form the quotient homomorphism
[-]_{\approx}\colon\mathsf{Terms}\to{\mathcal{A}}
where the AIC-structure
{\mathcal{A}}
is carried by
A=\mathsf{Terms}/\approx
.

Let us check that
{\mathcal{A}}\models\mathsf{Ax}
.
Let
P=(\bigwedge_{j\in J}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}e^{{\kern-5.92923pt{j}\kern 3.71072pt}}_{{\kern-3.36073pt\kern 3.71072pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}e^{{\kern-5.92923pt{j}\kern 3.71072pt}}_{{\kern-3.36073pt\kern 3.71072pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}e^{{\kern-3.68091pt{j}\kern 2.0534pt}}_{{\kern-1.70341pt\kern 2.0534pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}e^{{\kern-3.09422pt{j}\kern 1.46672pt}}_{{\kern-1.11673pt\kern 1.46672pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}f^{{\kern-6.72475pt{j}\kern 4.50624pt}}_{{\kern-4.15625pt\kern 4.50624pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}f^{{\kern-6.72475pt{j}\kern 4.50624pt}}_{{\kern-4.15625pt\kern 4.50624pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}f^{{\kern-4.26126pt{j}\kern 2.63376pt}}_{{\kern-2.28377pt\kern 2.63376pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{j}}}}f^{{\kern-3.50876pt{j}\kern 1.88126pt}}_{{\kern-1.53127pt\kern 1.88126pt}}})\Longrightarrow e=f
be an axiom in
\mathsf{Ax}
.

(

) is an interpretation that satisfies

By definition, this means that

of

.

in
\mathsf{Ax}
,
where by our definition of a derivation of a quasiequation, each

is allowed to have
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-5.35158pt{i}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-5.35158pt{i}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-3.3024pt{i}\kern 2.06718pt}}_{{\kern-1.7172pt\kern 2.06718pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-2.71178pt{i}\kern 1.47656pt}}_{{\kern-1.12657pt\kern 1.47656pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-4.67345pt{i}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-4.67345pt{i}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-2.82771pt{i}\kern 1.5925pt}}_{{\kern-1.24251pt\kern 1.5925pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-2.37271pt{i}\kern 1.1375pt}}_{{\kern-0.7875pt\kern 1.1375pt}}}
show up as a leaf for any

.
This derivation is well-founded because

is well-founded for each
j\in J
.
9
9

9

We feel it is important to note that this is where well-foundedness plays a role.
If we instead altered our definition of derivation to require
finite height
instead of well-foundedness, then this step no longer goes through: if the set of derivations
D_{j}
has no upper bound on their heights (say,
J=\omega
and the height of
D_{j}
is
j
like in
\cref
eq:infinite but wf), then we could no longer form the derivation in (
53
).

It follows that
\mathsf{Ax}\vdash_{\mathsf{wf}}(\bigwedge_{i\in I}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-5.35158pt{i}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-5.35158pt{i}\kern 3.68228pt}}_{{\kern-3.33229pt\kern 3.68228pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-3.3024pt{i}\kern 2.06718pt}}_{{\kern-1.7172pt\kern 2.06718pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}s^{{\kern-2.71178pt{i}\kern 1.47656pt}}_{{\kern-1.12657pt\kern 1.47656pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-4.67345pt{i}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-4.67345pt{i}\kern 3.00415pt}}_{{\kern-2.65416pt\kern 3.00415pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-2.82771pt{i}\kern 1.5925pt}}_{{\kern-1.24251pt\kern 1.5925pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{i}}}}t^{{\kern-2.37271pt{i}\kern 1.1375pt}}_{{\kern-0.7875pt\kern 1.1375pt}}})\Longrightarrow e[\sigma]=f[\sigma]
, and therefore by definition,

and thus
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{e}\rrbracket^{{\kern-15.83064pt{\sigma}\kern 13.1607pt}}_{{\kern-12.81071pt\kern 13.1607pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{e}\rrbracket^{{\kern-15.83064pt{\sigma}\kern 13.1607pt}}_{{\kern-12.81071pt\kern 13.1607pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{e}\rrbracket^{{\kern-10.61835pt{\sigma}\kern 8.6684pt}}_{{\kern-8.3184pt\kern 8.6684pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{e}\rrbracket^{{\kern-8.14165pt{\sigma}\kern 6.1917pt}}_{{\kern-5.8417pt\kern 6.1917pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{f}\rrbracket^{{\kern-16.62616pt{\sigma}\kern 13.95622pt}}_{{\kern-13.60623pt\kern 13.95622pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{f}\rrbracket^{{\kern-16.62616pt{\sigma}\kern 13.95622pt}}_{{\kern-13.60623pt\kern 13.95622pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{f}\rrbracket^{{\kern-11.1987pt{\sigma}\kern 9.24875pt}}_{{\kern-8.89876pt\kern 9.24875pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{f}\rrbracket^{{\kern-8.55618pt{\sigma}\kern 6.60623pt}}_{{\kern-6.25624pt\kern 6.60623pt}}}
.
This proves that
{\mathcal{A}}\models P
.
Since
P
was an arbitrary axiom of
\mathsf{Ax}
, we conclude that
{\mathcal{A}}\models\mathsf{Ax}
.

Now, by our assumption that

implies

, we infer that
{\mathcal{A}}\models\textup{{q}}
.

for each
i
, and from this we infer that
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{s}\rrbracket^{{\kern-15.8022pt{\sigma}\kern 13.13226pt}}_{{\kern-12.78227pt\kern 13.13226pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{s}\rrbracket^{{\kern-15.8022pt{\sigma}\kern 13.13226pt}}_{{\kern-12.78227pt\kern 13.13226pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{s}\rrbracket^{{\kern-10.63213pt{\sigma}\kern 8.68217pt}}_{{\kern-8.33218pt\kern 8.68217pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{s}\rrbracket^{{\kern-8.15149pt{\sigma}\kern 6.20154pt}}_{{\kern-5.85155pt\kern 6.20154pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{t}\rrbracket^{{\kern-15.12407pt{\sigma}\kern 12.45413pt}}_{{\kern-12.10414pt\kern 12.45413pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{t}\rrbracket^{{\kern-15.12407pt{\sigma}\kern 12.45413pt}}_{{\kern-12.10414pt\kern 12.45413pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{t}\rrbracket^{{\kern-10.15744pt{\sigma}\kern 8.20749pt}}_{{\kern-7.8575pt\kern 8.20749pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\sigma}}}}\llbracket{t}\rrbracket^{{\kern-7.81242pt{\sigma}\kern 5.86247pt}}_{{\kern-5.51248pt\kern 5.86247pt}}}
.
In other words,
s\approx t
, which is to say that
\mathsf{Ax}\vdash_{\mathsf{wf}}\textup{{q}}
.
∎

H.2
Completeness of
\boldsymbol{{\mathsf{AIC}}_{\omega}}

We are now ready to give the details behind the proof of
\Cref
thm:embedding.
We begin with the following result, which is stated without a proof in
[
40
, Proposition 3.3]
.
Admittedly, we found it less “completely straightforward” than Theunissen and Venema, so we have included a proof for the sake of completeness.

Lemma 53

(Monotone Lifting)
.

Let
N
be any lattice.
There is a complete lattice
L
and an order-embedding
10
10

10

A monotone map between posets
f\colon X\to Y
is an
order-embedding
if for any
x,x^{\prime}\in X
,
x\leq x^{\prime}
if and only if
f(x)\leq f(x^{\prime})
. Order-embedding maps are necessarily injective, and surjective order-embeddings are order isomorphisms.

m\colon N\hookrightarrow L
that preserves all meets and joins that exist in
N
such that for any monotone map
F\colon N\to N
, there is a monotone
\bar{F}\colon L\to L
such that
\bar{F}\circ m=m\circ F
.

Proof.

The lattice we have in mind is the
Dedekind-MacNeille completion
of
N

[
29
]
, which can be described as follows.
For each subset
V\subseteq N
, define

V^{\Delta}=\{x\in N\mid\forall v\in V,v\leq x\}\hskip 18.49988ptV^{\nabla}=\{x\in N\mid\forall v\in V,x\leq v\}

Then the operation
C\colon 2^{|N|}\to 2^{|N|}
defined
CV=(V^{\Delta})^{\nabla}
is a closure operation.
This gives rise to the complete lattice
L=\{V\in 2^{|N|}\mid V=CV\}
, ordered by set inclusion, and the embedding
m\colon N\to L
defined
m(v)=C\{v\}
.
It is known that the embedding
m\colon N\to L
preserves all meets and joins that exist in
N
.
It is worth noting that
(-)^{\Delta}
and
(-)^{\nabla}
are both antitone, and that this implies that
C=(-)^{\nabla}\circ(-)^{\Delta}
is monotone.

Now let
F\colon N\to N
be a monotone map.
We extend
F
to
L
by defining
\bar{F}\colon L\to L
by
\bar{F}(V)=C(F[V])
.
The operation
\bar{F}
is clearly monotone.
We are left with showing that
\bar{F}\circ m=m\circ F
.
Observe that for any
v\in N
,

\displaystyle m(v)=C\{v\}=(\{v\}^{\Delta})^{\nabla}=\{x\in N\mid\forall y\in\{v\}^{\Delta},x\leq y\}

\displaystyle=\{x\in N\mid\forall y\in{\uparrow}v,x\leq y\}=\{x\in N\mid x\leq v\}={\downarrow}v

The second to last equality can be seen by taking
y=v
.
In particular,
m\circ F(v)=C\{F(v)\}={\downarrow}F(v)
for any
v\in N
.
On the other side, we have

\bar{F}\circ m(v)=CF[C\{v\}]=CF[{\downarrow}v]=C\{F(x)\mid x\leq v\}

Therefore, our goal is to show that
C\{F(x)\mid x\leq v\}={\downarrow}F(v)
.
This will happen in two parts, one for each inclusion.

Given any
x\leq v
, by monotonicity we see that
F(x)\leq F(v)
.
It follows that
\{F(x)\mid x\leq v\}\subseteq{\downarrow}F(v)
.
Since
{\downarrow}F(v)\in L
, by monotonicity of
C
we have

C\{F(x)\mid x\leq v\}\subseteq C({\downarrow}F(v))={\downarrow}F(v)

This establishes one inclusion.

For the opposite inclusion, let
y\leq F(v)
.
We want to show that
y\in C\{F(x)\mid x\leq v\}=(\{F(x)\mid x\leq v\}^{\Delta})^{\nabla}
, which is equivalent to the statement that
for any
u\in N
such that
F(x)\leq u
for all
x\leq v
, we have
y\leq u
.
So, let
u\in N
such that
F(x)\leq u
for all
x\leq v
.
Then in particular,
F(v)\leq u
.
But
y\leq F(v)
.
This tells us that
y\leq u
, so
y\in C\{F(x)\mid x\leq v\}
as desired.
∎

See
27

Proof.

Start by constructing the
lattice of heads
,

L_{0}=\left\{{\circleddash}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}a~\middle|~a\in A_{0}\right\}

This is a lattice with lattice operations interpreted as

\displaystyle\bot^{L_{0}}={\circleddash}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}\bot^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\top^{L_{0}}={\circleddash}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}\top^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}

\displaystyle({\circleddash}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}a)\sqcap^{L_{0}}({\circleddash}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}b)={\circleddash}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}(a\curlywedge^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}b)\hskip 18.49988pt({\circleddash}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}a)\sqcup^{L_{0}}({\circleddash}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}b)={\circleddash}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}(a\curlyvee^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}b)

because
{\circleddash}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}
commutes with the lattice operations (see
\Cref
tab:complete AIC).
This allows us to further define the lattice homomorphism
e^{\prime}\colon A_{0}\to L_{0}^{\omega}
given by

e^{\prime}(a)(n)={\circleddash}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}({\medcirc}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}})^{n}a

where
({\medcirc}\,^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}})^{n}
is the
n
-fold application of the shift operation. That
e^{\prime}
is a lattice homomorphisms folllows from the Head and Shift axioms, which ensure the the
{\circleddash}\,
and
{\medcirc}\,
operations preserve commute with the lattice structure. It follows directly from the first sequence axiom in
\Cref
tab:complete AIC that
e^{\prime}
is injective.
Note that the function operations
F^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}
for
F\in\mathsf{Funcs}
restrict to
L_{0}
, since
F^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}{\circleddash}\,a={\circleddash}\,F^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}a
.

By
\Cref
lem:complete extension, there is a complete lattice
L
and an order-embedding
m\colon L_{0}\hookrightarrow L
such that every monotone operation
G\colon L_{0}\to L_{0}
extends to a monotone operation
\bar{G}\colon L\to L
.
Write
m^{\omega}\colon L_{0}^{\omega}\to L^{\omega}
for the pointwise embedding defined by
m^{\omega}(\sigma)(n)=m(\sigma(n))
for any
\sigma\in L_{0}^{\omega}
and
n\in\omega
.
We obtain a sequence algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
carried by
L^{\omega}
by lifting each operation
F^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}\colon L_{0}\to L_{0}
to a monotone operation
\bar{F}
on
L
and applying it pointwise to sequences to obtain the unary operation
F^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}}}\colon L^{\omega}\to L^{\omega}
.

From here on, most superscripts indicating the type of the operations will be supressed, since they can be inferred from context.

Finally, let
\varepsilon=m^{\omega}\circ e^{\prime}
.
We obtain the following diagram:

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hskip 18.49988pt\varepsilon(a)(n)=m^{\omega}\circ e^{\prime}(a)(n)=m({\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n}a)

It follows from the injectivity of
m^{\omega}
and
e^{\prime}
that
\varepsilon
is injective.
It now suffices to see that
\varepsilon
is a homomorphism of AIC-structures.

Lattice

Since
m
is a lattice homomorphism, so is the pointwise application
m^{\omega}
. Therefore
\varepsilon
is a lattice homomorphism, being the composite of the lattice homomorphisms
e^{\prime}
and
m^{\omega}
.

Head

Given
a\in A_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon({\circleddash}\,a)(n)

\displaystyle=m({\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n}{\circleddash}\,a)

\displaystyle=m({\circleddash}\,{\circleddash}\,a)

(Head)

\displaystyle=m({\circleddash}\,a)

(Head)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(a)(0)

(definition of
\varepsilon
)

\displaystyle=({\circleddash}\,\varepsilon(a))(n)

(sequence algebra)

Shift

Given
a\in A_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon({\medcirc}\,a)(n)

\displaystyle=m({\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n}{\medcirc}\,a)

\displaystyle=m({\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n+1}a)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(a)(n+1)

(definition of
\varepsilon
)

\displaystyle=({\medcirc}\,\varepsilon(a))(n)

(sequence algebra)

Functions

Given
a\in A_{0}
and
F\in\mathsf{Funcs}
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon(Fa)(n)

\displaystyle=m({\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n}Fa)

\displaystyle=m({\circleddash}\,F{\medcirc}\,^{n}a)

(Shift)

\displaystyle=m(F{\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n}a)

(Head)

\displaystyle=\bar{F}(m({\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n}a))

(
\bar{F}
extends
F
)

\displaystyle=\bar{F}(\varepsilon(a)(n))

(definition of
\varepsilon
)

\displaystyle=(F^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}}}\varepsilon(a))(n)

(sequence algebra)

Iteration

Given
a\in A_{0}
and
F\in\mathsf{Funcs}
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon(F^{*}a)(n)

\displaystyle=m({\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n}F^{*}a)

\displaystyle=m({\circleddash}\,F^{n}F^{*}{\medcirc}\,^{n}a)

(Shift)

\displaystyle=m(F^{n}{\circleddash}\,F^{*}{\medcirc}\,^{n}a)

(Head)

\displaystyle=m(F^{n}{\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n}a)

(Head)

\displaystyle=\bar{F}^{n}\circ m({\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n}a)

(
\bar{F}
lifts
F^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}}
)

\displaystyle=\bar{F}^{n}(\varepsilon(a)(n))

(definition of
\varepsilon
)

\displaystyle=((F^{*})^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathcal{A}}}}\varepsilon(a))(n)

(sequence algebra)

Majorum/Minorum

We will just do the majorum case (tail-suprema), because the minorum case is symmetric.
We begin with the following observation: for any
k\in\omega
and
c\in A_{0}
, by the Maj/Minorum and Shift axioms,

{\medcirc}\,^{k}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,c\sqsubseteq{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,c
.
We therefore have
{\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{k}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,c\sqsubseteq{\circleddash}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,c
for all
k\in\omega
.
The Sequence axioms now tell us that
{\circleddash}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,c
is the
least
upper bound of
\{{\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{k}c\mid k\in\omega\}
, and that this least upper bound exists in
L_{0}
.
Now, if we let
c={\medcirc}\,^{n}a
, we see that

\displaystyle{\circleddash}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n}a

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{k\geq 0}{\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{k}{\medcirc}\,^{n}a

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{k\geq 0}{\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n+k}a

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{k\geq n}{\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{k}a

Since
m
preserves all suprema that exist in
L_{0}
, we can derive

\displaystyle\varepsilon({\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a)(n)

\displaystyle=m({\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a)

\displaystyle=m({\circleddash}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,{\medcirc}\,^{n}a)

(Shift)

\displaystyle=m\Big(\bigsqcup_{k\geq n}{\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{k}a\Big)

(
{\circleddash}\,{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,a
a least upper bound)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{k\geq n}m({\circleddash}\,{\medcirc}\,^{k}a)

(
m
preserves joins)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{k\geq n}\varepsilon(a)(k)

(definition of
\varepsilon
)

\displaystyle=({\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\Diamond$}}\,\varepsilon(a))(k)

(sequence algebra)

Hence,
\varepsilon
is an algebra homomorphism, completing the proof of the embedding theorem.
∎
